2025-06-17T18:27:56.3400924Z *This section includes details on the pull request with code changes you have already submitted to fix the problem above.*
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3402018Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3402161Z <pr_title>
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3402679Z Simplify CI: consolidate bloated workflows and add immediate wheel access
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3403404Z </pr_title>
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3403601Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3403747Z <pr_description>
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3405090Z This PR addresses the CI complexity and reliability issues by dramatically simplifying the workflow structure and providing multiple access methods for package installation.
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3406468Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3406619Z ## Problem
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3407137Z The repository had **6 CI workflow files** with significant redundancy:
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3407875Z - `build-artifacts.yaml`
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3408352Z - `build-packages-dev.yaml` 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3408828Z - `build-packages-prod.yaml`
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3409320Z - `build-packages-staging.yaml`
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3409797Z - `create-release.yaml`
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3410367Z - `tools.yaml`
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3410577Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3411675Z This created maintenance complexity and CI reliability issues, making it difficult to successfully build and access wheel artifacts.
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3412706Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3412852Z ## Solution
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3413044Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3413214Z ### 1. Consolidated CI Workflows
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3413976Z **Reduced from 6 to 3 focused workflows** (50% reduction):
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3414443Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3415138Z - **`build-and-deploy.yaml`**: Unified workflow that builds once and deploys to appropriate environments based on triggers
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3416613Z - **`commit-wheels.yaml`**: Fallback workflow that commits wheels directly to the repository when CI builds succeed
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3417558Z - **`tools.yaml`**: Unchanged, handles build tools and container management
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3417854Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3417949Z ### 2. Multiple Installation Methods
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3418230Z Users can now install packages via:
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3418406Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3418502Z **One-line installer:**
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3418708Z ```bash
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3419207Z curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/install.sh | bash -s -- pandas
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3419851Z ```
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3419948Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3420240Z **Direct wheel download:**
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3420501Z ```bash
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3421160Z curl -L https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/wheels/pandas-1.5.0-cp310-cp310-linux_armv7l.whl -o /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3421869Z pip install /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3422090Z ```
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3422184Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3422331Z **CI artifacts and releases** (when CI runs successfully)
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3422575Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3422669Z ### 3. Immediate Package Access
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3423245Z Added pre-built wheel files directly to the repository in the `wheels/` directory, so packages are immediately available even if CI fails.
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3423754Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3423833Z ## Key Benefits
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3424078Z - **Simplified maintenance**: 50% fewer workflow files
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3424578Z - **Eliminated redundancy**: Single build logic instead of duplicated across multiple files
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3425091Z - **Improved reliability**: Multiple fallback access methods
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3425574Z - **Better user experience**: Immediate access to packages via curl/pip as requested
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3426132Z - **Clear documentation**: Added `CI-SIMPLIFICATION.md` explaining the changes
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3426449Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3426821Z The solution directly addresses the "bloated" CI concern while ensuring reliable package access through multiple channels.
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3427287Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3427363Z Fixes #11.
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3427466Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3427469Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3427553Z </pr_description>

2025-06-17T18:27:56.3507119Z Based on the Tailscale OAuth documentation, here are the customized setup instructions for your GitHub Actions integration:
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3507590Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3507700Z ## Tailscale OAuth Client Setup Instructions
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3507903Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3507983Z ### 1. Create OAuth Client
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3508479Z 1. Go to the [OAuth clients page](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/settings/oauth) in your Tailscale admin console
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3509005Z 2. Click **Generate OAuth client**
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3509463Z 3. Select the required scopes for GitHub Actions (recommend `auth_keys` scope for node registration)
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3510112Z 4. Add the `tag:ci` tag (or create a custom tag for GitHub Actions)
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3510453Z 5. Click **Generate client**
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3510948Z 6. **Important**: Copy both the Client ID and Client Secret immediately - you won't be able to view the secret again
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3511364Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3511447Z ### 2. Add GitHub Secrets
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3511719Z Add these to your repository's GitHub Actions secrets:
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3512052Z - `TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`: Your OAuth client ID
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3512352Z - `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`: Your OAuth client secret  
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3512557Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3512644Z ### 3. Repository Configuration
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3513727Z The `.github/workflows/copilot-setup-steps.yml` file is already configured with the Tailscale GitHub Action (currently commented out). Once you add the secrets above, I can uncomment and activate the Tailscale connection for OT-2 simulator access.
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3514639Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3514722Z ### 4. Verification
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3515399Z After setup, the workflow will automatically connect to your Tailscale network using the `tag:ci` tag, allowing secure access to your OT-2 simulator for testing Prefect installations.
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3516150Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3516528Z Let me know once you've completed the OAuth client setup and added the GitHub secrets, and I'll activate the Tailscale integration.

2025-06-17T18:27:56.3521264Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3521537Z Based on the Tailscale documentation, here's how to update your ACLs to add the new `tag:ci` tag:
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3521909Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3521991Z ## ACL JSON Configuration
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3522137Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3522346Z Add the tag to your `tagOwners` section in the Tailscale admin console ACL editor:
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3522664Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3522742Z ```json
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3522904Z {
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3523064Z   "tagOwners": {
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3523272Z     "tag:ci": ["your-email@domain.com"]
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3523522Z   },
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3523680Z   "acls": [
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3523845Z     {
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3524016Z       "action": "accept",
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3524235Z       "src": ["tag:ci"],
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3524441Z       "dst": ["*:*"]
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3524628Z     }
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3524785Z   ]
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3524940Z }
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3525092Z ```
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3525184Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3525262Z ## Admin Console Steps
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3525398Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3525681Z 1. Go to [Access Controls](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/acls) in your Tailscale admin console
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3526179Z 2. Add the `tag:ci` entry to your `tagOwners` section 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3526681Z 3. Optionally add ACL rules for the tag (the example above allows `tag:ci` devices to access anything)
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3527137Z 4. Save the policy file
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3527277Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3527365Z ## OAuth Client Configuration
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3527526Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3527980Z The OAuth client you created should automatically work with any tags defined in your ACL. No additional admin console configuration is needed beyond:
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3528534Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3528747Z 1. Creating the OAuth client with appropriate scopes (`auth_keys` recommended)
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3529210Z 2. Adding the `tag:ci` tag to your ACL policy as shown above
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3529634Z 3. Adding the GitHub secrets (`TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` and `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`)
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3529918Z 
2025-06-17T18:27:56.3530539Z The Tailscale GitHub Action will use the OAuth client to authenticate and automatically apply the `tag:ci` tag to the GitHub Actions runner when it connects to your tailnet.

2025-06-17T18:37:07.1887862Z       - `asyncpg` (PostgreSQL async driver) - requires compilation
2025-06-17T18:37:07.1888728Z       - `cryptography` (real version with proper encryption) - requires compilation

2025-06-17T18:37:07.1891546Z       ### 🎯 The Core Problem:
2025-06-17T18:37:07.1892691Z       Prefect is designed as a client-server architecture where flows need to connect to a Prefect server (even if local). When a flow runs, it tries to:
2025-06-17T18:37:07.1893790Z       1. Initialize a client context
2025-06-17T18:37:07.1894289Z       2. Connect to server components  
2025-06-17T18:37:07.1894716Z       3. Import server database models
2025-06-17T18:37:07.1894989Z       4. Set up encryption utilities
2025-06-17T18:37:07.1895368Z       
2025-06-17T18:37:07.1895967Z       This is happening because Prefect v3.3.4 (full version) includes both client and server components, while we need just the client-side execution.
2025-06-17T18:37:07.1896689Z       
2025-06-17T18:37:07.1897054Z       ### 🛠️ Potential Solutions:
2025-06-17T18:37:07.1897520Z       1. **Use prefect-client package**: We installed this earlier but it may not be properly configured
2025-06-17T18:37:07.1898260Z       2. **Environment Variables**: Set Prefect to run in offline/local mode
2025-06-17T18:37:07.1899041Z       3. **Mock Additional Dependencies**: Create mock versions of asyncpg and other server dependencies
2025-06-17T18:37:07.1900409Z       4. **Alternative Workflow Libraries**: Consider lighter alternatives like Dask or simple function orchestration
